# Component Library Experiments And Evidence

Lifecycle: **Active evidence companion**. Owner: Gabriel Bourget. Reviewed: **2026-10-18**. [Roadmap](roadmap.md) owns current tasks, contracts, catalogue and acceptance gates. This file records bounded experiment outcomes and historical lookup; raw logs and captures stay in private evidence packets. A historical failure, source checkpoint or passing repeat retains its actual proof tier.

<a id="catalogue-acceptance-matrix"></a>

## 3. Catalogue Acceptance Matrix

This is the active M2 completion ledger for all 53 stable catalogue IDs. Gate definitions, exact scenario IDs and required platform profiles live in [the acceptance rubric](roadmap.md#31-acceptance-rubric). A cell ID is `CAT.<manifest-id>.G<n>`; native scenario cells extend it with platform/profile/scenario/case as specified there. Every mandatory capability and scenario must be instantiated and resolved before its aggregate gate can pass. An empty or uninstantiated scenario set is not vacuous success.

`OPEN` is an administrative state before reconciliation/execution; it is not a failed test. Final verification dispositions are `PASS`, `FAIL`, `BLOCKED`, `N/A(reason)` or `OWNER-DEFERRED(authority)`. Historical passes retain their recorded scope and artifacts; opening a broader aggregate cell does not invalidate those receipts or promote them to full current-matrix acceptance. Source availability is tracked in the roadmap catalogue separately. The table initially leaves unreconciled aggregates open rather than inventing complete proof from earlier test counts.

Current required native scenario projection for CircularProgress, Counter, partial Carousel, supplemental Button/Text typography, shared ClickPopover host obligations and private Tooltip/HoverPopover: [section3.31.3](#3313-private-tooltip-surfaces-and-required-native-matrix), source producer 5ffa805, preserves all 3,910 CP-44 cells and 400 old-to-current ID aliases unchanged while adding 3,396 explicit tooltip/hover obligations. All 7,306 projected installed cells remain OPEN/null with 0 native actions. This remains a supplemental projection rather than the whole 53-component matrix; other families retain their checkpoint matrices and unresolved aggregate cells. CP-50 in 3.31.24 is the latest scoped public consumer/Release linkage proof for f86d8a6. Private Pagination construction and every remaining native acceptance obligation stay open. The 110 activation/release installed cells and private Breadcrumbs runtime remain OPEN. The 43 new relationship obligations also remain OPEN, with one platform-specific N/A; this projection does not replace the whole 53-component matrix. The 73 new navigation/current metadata obligations also remain OPEN, with three platform-specific N/A fields. Later source changes require their own matching proof.

#### 3.9.1. Installed Android Assertions And Oracle Disposition

One read-only API36/x86_64 guest is used for the whole run at 720×1600/density 280, logical app window 411×914 and fontScale 1. One observed stock System UI ANR Wait restores the same guest; it is not an app failure or an emulator restart.

Tag passes actual initial state and 48-unit target, first activation, two controlled-owner vetoes, numeric 0/string "0"/empty/undefined requests, disabled suppression, programmatic selection while disabled, held-press owner-disabled takeover without a fabricated request, and false/mixed exposed-state overrides. These receipts record native hierarchy/target fields, exact owner/request text, observed touch coordinates and the matched source/APK. The partial glyph/geometry captures are not promoted to full style/profile closure.

The original Tag physical-zero oracle fails at 9.714×10.286 native units. A real browser fixture using the exact canonical theme, compatibility bridge and Tag CSS measures 10×10 for authored 0px dimensions with 5px padding under UA/default, border-box and content-box sizing; zero insets alone produce 0×0. The native result equals 17×18 physical pixels at 1.75 density, matching the padding-box minimum within one physical pixel. The original failed expectation remains raw; it is reviewed as an invalid physical-zero oracle rather than relabelled passing or repaired by changing the library. The independent zero-padding-box assertion passes, and focused scene test1dfa4d2 now separates authored zero values from observed padding-box geometry. The decimal parser's consumed sentence punctuation and an overly exact 30-unit Avatar expectation are separately retained observer defects; actual 29.714 units is 52 physical pixels, within the declared pixel-grid tolerance. None is treated as a product workaround.

The original Avatar passes native frame/first touch through right-side automatic hitSlop/disabled suppression and local PNG decode/error rendering. It then fails a real passive-state assertion: caller state says Passive/Enabled, but the native ImageView has enabled=false because a disabled Pressable is used to suppress interaction. Source 425dd6f uses a passive View, retaining decoded state above host-mode changes, URI retirement guards and actual root refs. Two new regressions first fail before repair; forty Avatar cases subsequently pass. A separately verified corrected APK builds in 65.899s and cold-launches in 3.301s on the same owned guest. Matched native frame/hitSlop/disabled/passive/zero callbacks and valid/invalid PNG pixel assertions pass. The corrected passive ImageView is enabled=true/clickable=false. The failed original APK and its verdict remain unchanged.

The original 1000ms fallback-delay before-capture takes 1.036632912s from host dispatch and cannot prove the pre-eligibility window. That observer condition is BLOCKED, not a component failure or a reason for another startup cycle. Source timing cases remain source evidence. The corrected producer is tested for real PNG success/error independently, rather than borrowing the original producer's passes.

Normal-portrait Tag default long text wraps into two complete lines at 358.857×50.286; at authored width 140 it wraps into six complete lines at 140×116.571. Agent inspection confirms complete glyphs and an additional actual-end scroll/capture proves the final explanatory content is inside the viewport. The quiet frame counter is stable. This establishes the exact native wrapping/reachability facts; it does not establish canonical web nowrap equivalence or close other modes/styles/large-font/rotation/synthetic-gap profiles. That capability remains required OPEN under 3.1.1/3.1.4.

#### 3.21.1. Native Inset Precedence Repair

Native Button's round recipe includes paddingHorizontal7; the wrapper's padding5 or caller padding0 alone leaves that axis value intact. The complete pinned RAC ProgressBar/React Aria useProgressBar declarations and runtime, canonical CircularProgress/helpers/Path/CSS/receipt and existing suites are rechecked for the inherited valueLabel gap. #### 3.31.7. Scoped Native Key And Focus Binding

Source producer7d6b4eeb343e9c85a0a5d6dbb0e33307ec9b1325 binds NativeFloatingLayerHost's existing native root to the private CUIKeyboardScope, preserving its actual View ref, layout/style/event forwarding and React context without an extra native layout wrapper. Native target/modifier/editable/composition rules consume owned defaults before asynchronous JS; the JS lease model requires actual-window epoch and installed-policy acknowledgment, current eligibility and original consumed event identity. Callback-only or equivalent token-order updates preserve a lease; changed ownership, target, composition policy, guard, window/epoch or policy revision retires old packets. #### 3.31.12. Native Activation And Release

Gabriel approves DEC.native-activation.01 on 2026-10-18. The implementation exports the third native activation event arm and its source constants, adds semantic onPressUp and preserves raw press-in/out as touch-or-keyboard interactions. Android emits from its actual click listener; iOS emits from accessibilityActivate, calls the inherited accessibility callback separately and reports the activation handled. Observation time is read at the native callback, with no fabricated touch coordinates, key codes or hardware timestamp. Both bindings retain the real native target/ref. Private monotonically increasing activation IDs reject duplicate/retired packets; hosted delivery checks native owner/window/epoch against the acknowledged scope. Native callbacks require an active attached window. Standalone queued delivery across actual window replacement/loss still requires installed proof and is not closed by these source checks.

| 3.1.2 ownership/guards and current callbacks      | consumes %s activation without replay after re-enabling and uses the current callback, pending/disabled; retires held touch release during %s takeover without losing fresh native activation                                                                        | Guarded records stay consumed; held cycles cannot revive; fresh records and current callbacks work.                                                |
| 3.1.3 admission/lifetime                          | rejects foreign ownership, invalid kind/source/serial and retained callbacks after unmount; admits only the current same-window native scope session across deactivation/reactivation                                                                                | Foreign/retired/duplicate packets and inactive or old window sessions cannot act.                                                                  |
| 3.1.3 held input and release                      | finishes an admitted held pointer once and suppresses its later SDK action; ignores native activation during a held keyboard cycle and preserves genuine release order; delivers actual pointer release once with press-in delay %i                                  | No duplicate action/up; real release object and ordering survive delay ed activation.                                                              |
| 3.1.3 cancellation/long press/default policy      | does not deliver press-up for native key cancellation or pointer termination; retains actual release for a long press without adding an ordinary action; keeps an owner key-up veto separate from semantic release and does not invent an SDK accessibility callback | Cancelled releases are inert; long press keeps its own action; raw veto suppresses action; original SDK accessibility events stay separate.        |
| 3.1.3 current window guard                        | rechecks current window admission after semantic release before invoking the action                                                                                                                                                                                  | Semantic release may deactivate the window; action then stays blocked and end remains balanced.                                                    |

On 2026-10-17 Gabriel requests a persistent component-delivery goal and approves the paired LinearProgress/Meter native contract in the roadmap. Further XCTest/LLDB startup investigations are excluded from this goal, while RN-TEXT-01 and the original ComboBox installed gate retain their prior outcomes.

Component commit `2b39444` adds LinearProgress and Meter with shared private range rendering and motion handling, plus public prop/ref/render-state types. Required controlled values drive bounded range geometry, accessibility and callback state without a local value mirror. The implementation preserves semantic/default and five existing order colors, independent native style slots, geometry/direction/dimensions, string value labels and host-owned Intl formatting. LinearProgress alone supports unknown progress. Core RN animations stop on replacement/unmount; Reduce Motion stays authoritative over a late initial preference query, with static presentation on unavailable preferences. Palette, pins, toolchain, sealed fixtures and signing are unchanged.

Verification passes 64 focused native component cases, all 26 canonical web baseline cases, two new maintained-scene composition cases and 17 retained NativeShowcase cases. Native package build, public contract types and normal native commit hooks pass. The source suite maps all thirteen assertion categories from each web root suite and adds range, formatting, ownership/ref/accessibility and motion cases; this is capability coverage, not a numeric parity claim. Initial test-authoring errors queried deliberately accessibility-hidden decorations, expected exact binary floating point and relied on an empty-string accessibility matcher; corrected tests inspect the hidden visual host props, use numeric tolerance and directly verify the explicit empty string. Type-case placement/mock overload errors are corrected before the passing checks. One initial planning commit hook used the bundled package manager and aborted before install; rerunning with the existing pinned Node/pnpm PATH passes the normal hooks without dependency changes.

Correction `f7783c2` follows final source review of a pinned-RN platform distinction: Android accepts the ARIA meter enum but its delegate maps it to no accessibility role, suppressing the progressbar fallback. The component uses progressbar compatibility explicitly on Android and retains the meter role on iOS; a targeted platform case verifies the Android binding and bounded value without inventing editability. This remains source evidence, not installed speech proof.

The maintained Progress/Meter scene and curated native reference accompany this source milestone. The documentation build passes all 19 pages and its generated native reference contains the new usage examples. Both source counterparts are available through the public native barrel, taking the catalogue to 18 of 53 source implementations. No installed Android/iOS, measured rendering/motion or physical screen-reader acceptance is claimed. No simulator, XCTest, LLDB, app rebuild, signing or frozen text-fixture mutation occurs. ## 9. Current Raw First/Warm Investigation

Status: **Transport recovered for two completed raw methods; both focused verdicts fail; RN-TEXT-01 remains open**. Planning consolidation commit `f0cb8e4` establishes this single roadmap/evidence structure, feature branch convention and normal formatter coverage. No production component is changed.

The detailed comparison preserves JS observation timestamps relative to the XCTest typeText call. They are not native mutation timestamps.

| Observation                                   |                       Original first raw failure |               Original passing warm raw |
| --------------------------------------------- | -----------------------------------------------: | --------------------------------------: |
| First key/native-change callback              |                         926.20 / 927.20 ms (`N`) |                    1209.69 / 1209.69 ms |
| Last native change                            |                               927.20 ms; count 1 |                    1212.69 ms; count 17 |
| Ref detach/reattach                           |            933.20 / 935.20 ms, same instance/tag | 1222.69 / 1225.69 ms, same instance/tag |
| Owner commit                                  |                                  935.20 ms (`N`) |                1225.69 ms (full phrase) |
| Async result replacement                      |                                 1064.20 ms (`N`) |                1354.69 ms (full phrase) |
| Last key callback                             | 1912.20 ms; 19 callbacks including two extra `N` |                1212.69 ms; 17 callbacks |
| Native typing call duration                   |                                       1921.40 ms |                              1232.80 ms |
| Heartbeat maximum gap through focused verdict |                                           118 ms |                                  118 ms |

The failed observation interleaves the first change, an owner commit and result replacement before the remaining key-callback batch. The passing observation delivers all 17 change callbacks before the owner commit/result replacement. This identifies an ordering difference worth isolating; it does not prove the render or async replacement lost characters, nor reconstruct UIKit application timing.

The exact installed RN 0.86.3 source was inspected and copied with SHA-256 identities into the private packet:

- Fabric RCTTextInputComponentView.mm emits onKeyPress inside textInputShouldChangeText before acceptance/change publication. Default maxLength is int max and the fixture sets no limit. Key callbacks therefore establish proposals at that boundary, not accepted/applied edits.
- RCTBackedTextInputDelegateAdapter.mm calls the proposal delegate, then sets textDidChangeIsComing when it accepts the same replacement. EditingChanged clears that flag and calls textInputDidChange. Its pending-change fallback on end editing is a possible branch, not observed execution in the original failure.
- Fabric textInputDidChange has JS-origin and next-change suppression branches; updateState/setTextAndSelection guard controlled feedback with event-count equality. Neither the original JS trace nor the later post-verdict capture can identify every branch taken during typing.
- TextInput.js rebuilds setLocalRef when mostRecentEventCount changes. useMergeRefs/useRefEffect can clean up and reassign the same instance when that callback changes. This supplies an upstream explanation for the recorded same-instance ref callbacks; those callbacks do not establish a native view remount. No Codon ref/synchronization patch is justified by their presence.

A private observer variant changes only the post-focused-verdict hold so both raw conditions can receive read-only UIKit/KVC snapshots. Its binary SHA-256 is 033de169c329e6697c1f070ea1ee9c9aa4bb233c687d8b2dd6da31e72d620de1. The Release application remains byte-identical; the observer is a distinct condition from prior inspector4a645f3d. The variant builds, and its snapshot expression passes a syntax check against the installed simulator SDK. Six result-parser cases preserve failed focused verdicts and exclude later debugger time, four native-session-progress cases pass, and six host-binding cases fail closed on wrong/reused process identities: **16 focused private cases pass**. These are helper/source checks, not new native input proof.

Before this continuation, the prelaunch guest-bridge PID lookup times out before xcodebuild starts. A verified same-user host PID/parent/path/birth binding removes that instrumentation dependency. The first actual preflight completes session initiation and runner installation, then stops at launch without an acquired runner PID or test-method verdict. After the one planned retained-guest shutdown/reboot recovery, its installer starts on demand; direct libproc metadata avoids an all-process-argument lookup that itself times out under host load. The final actual preflight remains in testmanagerd session initiation without a completed verdict.

| Actual native preflight                      | Controller outcome                                                                                                                  | Native input evidence                                                               |
| -------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| health-02                                    | Interrupted after 146.83 s for 90 s without stdout/native-session progress; no grace, exit -15, partial bundle/no completed verdict | None; no test method or raw attempt.                                                |
| health-03, after one retained-guest recovery | Interrupted after 112.53 s for the same silence criterion; no grace, exit -15, partial bundle/no completed verdict                  | None; no acquired PID, matching Codon child or raw attempt at the final host query. |

**The preceding run executes zero new raw cases and zero LLDB/native snapshots.** Its prepared two-case budget is unused because transport never passes. ## 10. LLDB Attachment And Native Trace

The next bounded run keeps the same retained 89459775 guest and frozen Release app. Its initial limit is 60 minutes, two owned host-probe attachments, three simulator attachments and two gated raw invocations. After the agent-authored live expression fails to compile, automatic approval review rejects a fourth attachment/third invocation as exceeding that bound. Gabriel then explicitly approves one bounded replay; the original deadline remains fixed. That approval authorizes one replay only and does not turn setup failures into typing evidence.

The owned unsigned macOS probe passes attach/register/scalar-read/detach in 1.52 s. Simulator attachment01 passes in 112.98 s with actual instruction-register/16-byte reads, unchanged PID/birth/executable identity and an untraced resumed process afterward. This establishes that the earlier 30-second timeout did not establish attachment denial. No signing, debugger entitlement or security reset is needed for those proofs.

Native stack samples locate successive expensive attachment stages: executable scripting-resource/dSYM discovery, BoundsSafety instrumentation-runtime symbol checks, then debugserver-requested symbol lookup through DWARF/Mach-O processing. The private session profile defers symbol/module/script discovery, disables external lookup and the debugger's BoundsSafety discovery plugin, and disables local-variable injection for expressions. These changes are confined to that LLDB process; they neither disable application checks nor prove generally fast attachment. Attachment02 hits its 180-second cap before native metadata or typing. Attachment03 completes attachment and UIKit imports, but its metadata expression fails compilation because Objective-C exceptions are disabled, untyped message sends are rejected and local names collide with injected symbols. Its outer elapsed time is 227.752 s; the field helper's 0.0166 s value measures reuse of an already stopped process, not actual attach latency. Cleanup detaches successfully, with zero typing and zero breakpoint events.

The corrected metadata expression uses runtime ivar lookup, explicit casts and prefixed local names, with no KVC exception handler or target setters. Its complete Objective-C++ wrapper passes an exceptions-disabled simulator-SDK syntax check using a private module cache. That is preparation evidence; live evaluation still has to pass before releasing the unchanged typing call. Prepared auto-continue address breakpoints observe proposal/change/selection/state/controlled-write paths with read-only arguments and counters, capped at 90 seconds/300 events. Native formatter reads must first demonstrate that they do not run the target. This is a separate observer condition whose scheduling can affect the burst; it cannot replace the frozen first/warm comparison.

A separate private preflight defect is isolated: the immediate Transport 1 assertion can run before the controlled owner update. The failed warm preflight's later screenshot/hierarchy already shows Transport 1. Only the private no-editor preflight gains a bounded 15-second exact-label expectation after its single tap; the raw focused assertion and unpaced typing remain unchanged. The rebuilt private observer then passes one actual callback test in 100.66 s with an exported Transport 1 attachment. The application is not rebuilt, and its 67 files remain frozen. This callback pass does not guarantee the next application launch.

The single approved replay completes with xcodebuild exit 65 after 216.76 s: testRaw fails in application launch with Timed out attempting to launch app, before opening the raw case or reaching the release gate. The completed result and failure attachments are exported. No fourth simulator attachment is started, no corrected metadata expression is evaluated live and no typing runs. The controller's attempted focused-result parse therefore has no raw verdict to parse; this is an application-launch failure, not a failed text assertion. No further replay follows.

Final native totals are one host-probe attachment, three simulator attachments, three gated raw invocations, zero typing calls, zero focused text verdicts and zero native breakpoint events. Basic LLDB read/detach is proven, but reliable XCTest startup and live metadata evaluation remain unproven. The prepared probe is not promoted as a validated native mutation tracer. RN-TEXT-01 remains open, and the prior frozen first/warm failures retain their original meaning. No new Input/ComboBox comparison or component acceptance is inferred. A future continuation must first pass reliable no-editor callback startup and a minimal live metadata read before committing another typing budget; no automatic continuation is authorized here.

The corrected runner_bootstrap_failure guard recognizes only the observed unentered AX/Busy conditions with exit65, no controller stop, no started/completed method and zero typing. Rejected configuration and the final unexplained bootstrap kill stop the loop. priority_callback_proof separately validates the original fixture's actual controlled-open-veto owner payload; a matched diagnostic Transport 1 attachment cannot substitute. Seven new focused cases cover that protocol and the warm-condition boundary; all 32 helper cases pass. Reclassification of the actual retained AX/Busy and configuration receipts also passes without rewriting them.

The final profile keeps supported test-host paths and requests optional checker instrumentation off. Exported native launch options confirm libRPAC and Main Thread Checker injection are present in the baseline and absent in that profile. The same runner remains live, runnable and untraced during its long connection gap; the debugger-wait flag alone does not establish a stopped process. None of the three supported acquisition failures reaches the 600/300-second outer XCTest limits. Both cold conditions record a separate 120-second control-session initiation timeout before later runner launch. The baseline subsequently obtains a successful new control-session reply, then fails AX initialization; the checker profile never finishes bootstrapping and is killed. No matching new host/guest crash report identifies the killer. A hash-verified copy of the preceding passing callback result has a successful control-session reply without this initial timeout. Different app/observer conditions limit that contrast; it does not establish a unique cause or certify a repair.

Aggregate totals are five xcodebuild replay commands: three supported runner-acquisition attempts and two configuration rejections. The earlier app-install cap is not an XCTest invocation. Selected methods, actual callback passes, priority acceptance methods, typing calls and focused editor verdicts are all zero. <a id="control-session-activation-and-bootstrap"></a>

### 10.7. Control-Session Activation And Bootstrap

The original controller-enforced 40-minute investigation starts at 00:25:00 Toronto time on 2026-10-17 (04:25:00 UTC), with a five-minute native cleanup reserve. Investigation ends at 00:44:39 and guest cleanup at 00:44:54, inside both limits. The initial single-boot plan gains one retained-guest boot for validation after sampling confounds the first bootstrap outcome; the original deadline is not extended. Two conditions reuse the original priority app, observer Swift/source and compiled products without rebuilding them. Controlled values, unpaced typing/assertions, keyboard defaults, pins, palette and deferred iPhone signing stay unchanged.

The retained guest advertises TESTMANAGERD_SIM_SOCK and an existing com.apple.testmanagerd job in its Background launch context. The job is loaded on demand with no PID. launchctl start activates that existing job before Xcode opens its timed control connection; no daemon is killed/reset and no launch configuration is changed. PID/UID/birth/executable binding, the exact runtime program, advertised socket and the guest launchd parent are checked. The first activation guard fails without retaining the rejected state; a later observation verifies the same daemon without reactivating it. The second preparation records observed states before checking liveness. A successful process observation remains preparation evidence, not callback proof.

| Condition                                                                   | Initial complete control reply | XCTest controller elapsed | Actual outcome                                                                                              |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------ | ------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Existing daemon started before Xcode; private runner later sampled          | 29 ms                          | 343.39 s                  | Runner is killed before its test-bundle connection/method. The sampling command overlaps its disappearance. |
| Same preparation on another retained-guest boot, without live runner probes | 17 ms                          | 24.15 s                   | SpringBoard denies runner launch as Busy / application failed preflight checks; no selected method.         |

Both replies contain the full capabilities object and null error; neither records a native control-session timeout. The historical failed condition retains its initial 120-second timeout and a distinct later 4 ms reconnection, while the preceding passing callback has a 4 ms initial reply. The new control_session.py reader preserves these separate exchanges and rejects socket-only, orphan, truncated, error, late-timeout and ambiguous-overlap interpretations. Midnight crossing and measured latency without an invented timeout are covered. Eight new cases bring the focused helper suite to 40 passing cases; real historical/current log replay also passes. Earlier analysis source/results remain preserved when the output field is clarified to completedWithoutRecordedTimeout. These observations do not establish a unique causal repair, PID authorization, AX readiness or the actual owner callback.

A read-only thread observation finds one private-runner thread in an uninterruptible wait, not a stopped state. A subsequent one-second sample returns exit0 while the runner disappears and produces no stack artifact. That is failed stack proof and a scheduling confound; this run cannot attribute the kill independently of sampling. The earlier unsampled bootstrap kill remains separate evidence. No matching new crash report or narrowly queried host log identifies the killer. Read-only inspection finds an Apple-signed runner and no quarantine attribute; signing/security are not changed. A later memory snapshot shows 8261.50 MiB of swap in use, while top collection is unavailable after a permission failure and capped read. These observations do not prove a unique resource cause. The validation without runner probes has no sampling/thread inspection and fails at launch instead.

Preparation and orchestration errors remain separate from XCTest: the copied seal verifier first rejects its stale parent root before writing, then is corrected within the fresh packet. The single-condition validation script also emits an unused warm-transition/cooldown message and runs a service-list query that reaches its 90-second cap; it creates no warm XCTest invocation. Neither actual XCTest failure reaches its 600/300-second total/silence allowance. Aggregate totals are two boots of the same retained guest, two existing-daemon activations, two XCTest invocations, zero new guests or service/security resets and zero LLDB attachments. Selected methods, actual callback passes, typing calls and focused editor verdicts are zero; acceptance never runs.

Final cleanup restores the exact diagnostic app67, stops all five task guests and closes only identified task Simulator UI. The original priority app67, installed private runner34, cached observer identities, source62/archive/locks/pins/palette and global Developer selection verify unchanged. Font preferences are not written or freshly queried. All 21 inherited manifests/10242 listed files verify as data; sealed helpers are never executed/imported. The fresh native-xctest-control-session-2026-10-17 packet under the existing chat evidence root retains the complete sources, activation/guard records, original failure receipts, sampling limitations, clean validation and cleanup. The initial control connection is quick in these prepared conditions, but native ComboBox acceptance and RN-TEXT-01 remain open at runner launch/test-bundle bootstrap and later AX/callback prerequisites. Another unchanged control-session replay is not the next gate.
